========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, um… I guess I’m here because things at home have been a lot harder to handle lately. My sister and her baby moved back home, and ever since then I feel like I’ve been more on edge than I normally am. Like little stuff around the house sets me off, and I’ll snap, and then afterward I’m thinking, “Why did I even react like that?”

And it’s not like I’m completely falling apart or anything—I’m still going to class and showing up at the shop—but I feel like I’m getting there already tired, like drained before the day even starts. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep track of everything I was supposed to do. It’s just been hard to keep my head straight with all of it.

Yeah… I’ve been feeling down more than usual, I guess. I don’t know if I’d say it’s every single minute or like I’m just completely sad all day, but lately it’s been there a lot more than I’m used to.

It really seems like it started after my sister and her baby moved back home. The house just feels more crowded and tense, and I’ve been getting worn out by it. I’ll snap over little things, then feel bad about it afterward, and by the time I get to class or the shop I already feel drained, like I don’t have much left in me. I’m still doing what I have to do mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.

So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, if I get a quiet moment, I can still enjoy something, but it takes me longer to actually settle into it. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and then realize I’m distracted or just kind of not as into it as I expected to be. But I don’t know if I’d say I can’t enjoy things at all. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. Like, I wouldn’t say there’s something I used to love and now I get zero pleasure from it. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.

I haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.

But I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.

So, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.

So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient experiencing Adjustment Disorder, in a diagnostic interview with a\npsychiatrist. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.

I mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.

So… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.

But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / industrial_or_refractory_machinery_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.

I’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.

So… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.

Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / industrial_or_refractory_machinery_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.

I guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.

So… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.

I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep track of everything I was supposed to do. It’s just been hard to keep my head straight with all of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if it’s “no reason,” exactly. It feels pretty tied to what’s been going on at home. Since my sister and her baby moved back in, I’ve just been more on edge than I’m used to. Like I’ll be sitting there trying to do homework or get ready for work at the shop, and little stuff around the house will set me off way more than it should.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m anxious out of nowhere all the time, but I do feel keyed up a lot lately, and kind of down and drained too. I’ll show up to class already tired, like I used up all my patience before the day started. I’m still getting through things, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I had to back out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve been feeling down more than usual, I guess. I don’t know if I’d say it’s every single minute or like I’m just completely sad all day, but lately it’s been there a lot more than I’m used to.\n\nIt really seems like it started after my sister and her baby moved back home. The house just feels more crowded and tense, and I’ve been getting worn out by it. I’ll snap over little things, then feel bad about it afterward, and by the time I get to class or the shop I already feel drained, like I don’t have much left in me. I’m still doing what I have to do mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, if I get a quiet moment, I can still enjoy something, but it takes me longer to actually settle into it. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and then realize I’m distracted or just kind of not as into it as I expected to be. But I don’t know if I’d say I can’t enjoy things at all. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. Like, I wouldn’t say there’s something I used to love and now I get zero pleasure from it. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient experiencing Adjustment Disorder, in a diagnostic interview with a\npsychiatrist. Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / industrial_or_refractory_machinery_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response\nmanifestation: \"I came in because ever since my sister and her baby moved back home, I’ve been more on edge and down than usual, snapping over little stuff at the house and then showing up to class or the shop already drained, so I’m still getting by but I’ve missed a couple deadlines and backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.

It’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.

With friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.

the shop already drained, so I’m still getting by but I’ve missed a couple deadlines and backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight.\n- key: month, since my sister and her baby moved back into the house.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 18 / industrial_or_refractory_machinery_mechanic / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S081\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.

Like with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.

So… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.

So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.\n\nLike with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”

I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.

My sister and her baby moved back home, and ever since then I feel like I’ve been more on edge than I normally am. Like little stuff around the house sets me off, and I’ll snap, and then afterward I’m thinking, “Why did I even react like that?”\n\nAnd it’s not like I’m completely falling apart or anything—I’m still going to class and showing up at the shop—but I feel like I’m getting there already tired, like drained before the day even starts. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep track of everything I was supposed to do. It’s just been hard to keep my head straight with all of it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I don’t know if it’s “no reason,” exactly. It feels pretty tied to what’s been going on at home. Since my sister and her baby moved back in, I’ve just been more on edge than I’m used to. Like I’ll be sitting there trying to do homework or get ready for work at the shop, and little stuff around the house will set me off way more than it should.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I’m anxious out of nowhere all the time, but I do feel keyed up a lot lately, and kind of down and drained too. I’ll show up to class already tired, like I used up all my patience before the day started. I’m still getting through things, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I had to back out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve been feeling down more than usual, I guess. I don’t know if I’d say it’s every single minute or like I’m just completely sad all day, but lately it’s been there a lot more than I’m used to.\n\nIt really seems like it started after my sister and her baby moved back home. The house just feels more crowded and tense, and I’ve been getting worn out by it. I’ll snap over little things, then feel bad about it afterward, and by the time I get to class or the shop I already feel drained, like I don’t have much left in me. I’m still doing what I have to do mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, if I get a quiet moment, I can still enjoy something, but it takes me longer to actually settle into it. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and then realize I’m distracted or just kind of not as into it as I expected to be. But I don’t know if I’d say I can’t enjoy things at all. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. Like, I wouldn’t say there’s something I used to love and now I get zero pleasure from it. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.\n\nLike with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wouldn’t say there’s been some obvious, major sleep problem that I can point to, like “yes, every night I’m awake until 3 a.m.” or anything like that. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. It’s more like normal regret in the moment, I guess. I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wouldn’t say there’s been some obvious, major sleep problem that I can point to, like “yes, every night I’m awake until 3 a.m.” or anything like that. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… a little. Not like I can’t function at all, but I’ve noticed I’m more scattered than I usually am. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just felt more hectic, and I think I’m carrying that with me. I’ll leave already kind of on edge or drained, and then by the time I’m in class or at the shop, I’m not as sharp.

Like I can still get through the day, but I’m making more small mistakes or forgetting what I was supposed to do next. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, at least not when I have things under control. And I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head — like scheduling, what I’d already committed to, what needed to get done. It started feeling like too much to organize.

So yes, I guess concentrating and deciding what to prioritize has been harder lately, but I’m still mostly getting by. It’s just taking more effort than it should.

I’ll show up to class already tired, like I used up all my patience before the day started. I don’t know if I’d say it’s every single minute or like I’m just completely sad all day, but lately it’s been there a lot more than I’m used to.\n\nIt really seems like it started after my sister and her baby moved back home. The house just feels more crowded and tense, and I’ve been getting worn out by it. I’ll snap over little things, then feel bad about it afterward, and by the time I get to class or the shop I already feel drained, like I don’t have much left in me. I’m still doing what I have to do mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, if I get a quiet moment, I can still enjoy something, but it takes me longer to actually settle into it. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and then realize I’m distracted or just kind of not as into it as I expected to be. But I don’t know if I’d say I can’t enjoy things at all. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. Like, I wouldn’t say there’s something I used to love and now I get zero pleasure from it. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.\n\nLike with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wouldn’t say there’s been some obvious, major sleep problem that I can point to, like “yes, every night I’m awake until 3 a.m.” or anything like that. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. It’s more like normal regret in the moment, I guess. I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble concentrating or making decisions in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. Not like I can’t function at all, but I’ve noticed I’m more scattered than I usually am. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just felt more hectic, and I think I’m carrying that with me. I’ll leave already kind of on edge or drained, and then by the time I’m in class or at the shop, I’m not as sharp.\n\nLike I can still get through the day, but I’m making more small mistakes or forgetting what I was supposed to do next. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, at least not when I have things under control. And I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head — like scheduling, what I’d already committed to, what needed to get done. It started feeling like too much to organize.\n\nSo yes, I guess concentrating and deciding what to prioritize has been harder lately, but I’m still mostly getting by. It’s just taking more effort than it should.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yeah, I guess I’d say I’m more apprehensive than I used to be, but not like I’m scared of normal things out of nowhere. It’s more like I’m already tense before I even get into the day.

So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that.
