It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.\n\nLike with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wouldn’t say there’s been some obvious, major sleep problem that I can point to, like “yes, every night I’m awake until 3 a.m.” or anything like that. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. It’s more like normal regret in the moment, I guess. I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble concentrating or making decisions in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. Not like I can’t function at all, but I’ve noticed I’m more scattered than I usually am. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just felt more hectic, and I think I’m carrying that with me. I’ll leave already kind of on edge or drained, and then by the time I’m in class or at the shop, I’m not as sharp.\n\nLike I can still get through the day, but I’m making more small mistakes or forgetting what I was supposed to do next. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, at least not when I have things under control. And I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head — like scheduling, what I’d already committed to, what needed to get done. It started feeling like too much to organize.\n\nSo yes, I guess concentrating and deciding what to prioritize has been harder lately, but I’m still mostly getting by. It’s just taking more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're often apprehensive or fearful about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I’d say I’m more apprehensive than I used to be, but not like I’m scared of normal things out of nowhere. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I mean… I guess I do feel kind of restless, but it’s more like I’m already wound up and can’t really settle down the way I normally would. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just been busier and louder, and I feel like I’m on edge a lot more. So even when I’m supposed to be relaxing, I’m not fully relaxed — I’m thinking about what’s going on at home, or what I’m behind on, or what I forgot to do.

I wouldn’t say I’m constantly pacing or anything like that, but I’ll notice myself shifting around, checking my phone, getting irritated by little noises, that kind of thing. And then I’ll snap over something small at the house, which makes me feel bad after, because it’s not really about that one thing.

It’s like I’m still getting through class and the shop, but I’m showing up already drained, and then everything takes more effort. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep all the scheduling and commitments straight in my head. So yeah, when I try to relax, I don’t feel very settled lately.

It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. It’s more like normal regret in the moment, I guess. I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble concentrating or making decisions in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.\n\nLike with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wouldn’t say there’s been some obvious, major sleep problem that I can point to, like “yes, every night I’m awake until 3 a.m.” or anything like that. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. It’s more like normal regret in the moment, I guess. I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble concentrating or making decisions in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. Not like I can’t function at all, but I’ve noticed I’m more scattered than I usually am. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just felt more hectic, and I think I’m carrying that with me. I’ll leave already kind of on edge or drained, and then by the time I’m in class or at the shop, I’m not as sharp.\n\nLike I can still get through the day, but I’m making more small mistakes or forgetting what I was supposed to do next. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, at least not when I have things under control. And I backed out of helping with the theater set because I just couldn’t keep everything straight in my head — like scheduling, what I’d already committed to, what needed to get done. It started feeling like too much to organize.\n\nSo yes, I guess concentrating and deciding what to prioritize has been harder lately, but I’m still mostly getting by. It’s just taking more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're often apprehensive or fearful about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I’d say I’m more apprehensive than I used to be, but not like I’m scared of normal things out of nowhere. It’s more like I’m already tense before I even get into the day.\n\nLike, ever since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just been… a lot. Not bad exactly, but louder, more crowded, more unpredictable. I’ll be at home and something small happens, and I snap or get irritated way faster than I normally would. Then I leave for class or the shop already feeling drained, and it’s like I’m bracing for everything to be harder.\n\nSo everyday stuff doesn’t feel terrifying, but it does feel like it takes more out of me than it should. I’m still getting by, mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. That kind of made me realize I’m not handling things as smoothly as I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Do you frequently find yourself restless or fidgety when you're trying to relax?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I guess I do feel kind of restless, but it’s more like I’m already wound up and can’t really settle down the way I normally would. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just been busier and louder, and I feel like I’m on edge a lot more. The house just feels more crowded and tense, and I’ve been getting worn out by it. I’ll snap over little things, then feel bad about it afterward, and by the time I get to class or the shop I already feel drained, like I don’t have much left in me. I’m still doing what I have to do mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, if I get a quiet moment, I can still enjoy something, but it takes me longer to actually settle into it. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and then realize I’m distracted or just kind of not as into it as I expected to be. But I don’t know if I’d say I can’t enjoy things at all. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. Like, I wouldn’t say there’s something I used to love and now I get zero pleasure from it. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not exactly, no. I mean, being away from home helps a little in the sense that I’m not right in the middle of the noise and the baby stuff and everyone moving around, so I can sometimes focus better for a bit. But I don’t feel like I just walk out the door and suddenly I’m back to normal.

It’s more like I’m carrying it with me. I’ll get to class or to the shop already tense or drained because something happened at home before I left, or because I’m thinking about what it’s going to be like when I get back. And then even normal things feel harder to keep straight. That’s kind of why I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and why I backed out of helping with the theater set. I wanted to do it, but I just couldn’t keep all the moving pieces organized in my head.

So yeah, away from home is maybe a little easier, but I wouldn’t say I feel like myself lately. I’m still functioning, but I’m more on edge and kind of down compared to how I usually am.

I’m still doing what I have to do mostly, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I backed out of helping with the theater set because I couldn’t keep everything straight. So yeah… not like I’m totally unable to function, but I’ve definitely been more down and on edge than I normally am.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying the things you usually like to do?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean, I don’t think everything I like is suddenly meaningless or anything like that. It’s more like I haven’t really had the same space in my head for it, if that makes sense.\n\nLike, if I get a quiet moment, I can still enjoy something, but it takes me longer to actually settle into it. Sometimes I’ll start doing something I normally like and then realize I’m distracted or just kind of not as into it as I expected to be. But I don’t know if I’d say I can’t enjoy things at all. It’s more… harder to get there lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not exactly. Like, I wouldn’t say there’s something I used to love and now I get zero pleasure from it. It’s more that I’m not always getting into things the same way, or I’ll be too distracted to really enjoy them the whole time.\n\nSo if I’m doing something I normally like, I can still have moments where it feels good or I laugh or I’m interested. It just doesn’t always come as easily, if that makes sense. I haven’t really noticed a specific thing where I’m like, “Yeah, I don’t care about that anymore.” It’s more uneven than gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about the future or things that might happen?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know. I probably worry some, but I’m not sure I’d say I’m constantly thinking about the future or imagining bad things happening. It’s more like my mind gets stuck on whatever is right in front of me, and then I’ll sort of jump ahead a little, like, “What if this keeps being stressful?” or “What if I can’t keep up?” But I don’t know if that’s the same as worrying about the future in the way you mean.\n\nI haven’t really noticed myself sitting around planning for disasters or anything like that. It’s not like I’m convinced something terrible is going to happen. It’s more just… when things feel unsettled, I start thinking too far ahead for a minute, and then I have to pull myself back. So yeah, maybe a little, but I’m not sure how much of that is normal stress versus something bigger.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain places, people, or activities because of how you've been feeling lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’m avoiding places exactly, like I’m not scared to go somewhere or anything like that. I’m still going to class, and I’m still showing up for work. So on the surface I guess I’m functioning.\n\nBut I have been kind of cutting back on things because I can’t keep up the way I normally would. Like I turned in a couple assignments late, which I don’t usually like doing, and I had to drop this community theater set project I was helping with. That one bothered me because it’s something I normally would’ve wanted to stick with, and I wasn’t trying to avoid the people there or the place or anything. It was more like I just felt too stretched and couldn’t keep all the pieces together.\n\nSo, I guess yes, in the sense that I’ve backed away from some activities. But not because I’m avoiding them exactly—more because I don’t feel like I have enough bandwidth lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely or anything—I don’t weigh myself every day, or even every week really—so I guess I can’t say with total certainty if it’s changed by a couple pounds. But I haven’t noticed my clothes fitting differently or anyone commenting on it.\n\nSo as far as I can tell, my weight’s been about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things even when there isn't anything specifically stressful happening?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “often,” no. Like, if nothing is really going on, I don’t usually just sit there worrying out of nowhere. At least I don’t think I do.\n\nI mean, sometimes my mind will still kind of wander into “what if” stuff, but it usually feels connected to something—even if it’s something small or something that happened earlier. It’s not like I wake up on a perfectly calm day and start worrying about random things for no reason. If I’m worrying, there’s usually some kind of stressor behind it, even if I’m maybe making it bigger in my head than it needs to be.\n\nSo… I guess no, not really. Or not in a way I’ve noticed as a pattern.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and won't go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in the way you mean. I mean, I do replay stuff sometimes, like if I said something awkward or if I’m thinking about something I need to deal with, but it’s not like a thought gets stuck in my head and I can’t make it stop no matter what.\n\nI’m trying to think of an example, because I don’t want to just say no too quickly. Sometimes I’ll go over the same thing a few times, but it usually feels more like regular thinking or overthinking, not like some unwanted thought that keeps forcing its way back in. If I get busy with something else or distract myself, it usually fades.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're frequently on the verge of panic or having panic attacks?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve definitely felt stressed or kind of worked up at times, but not like I’m suddenly about to lose control or like my body is going into some full panic thing.\n\nI guess when I hear “panic attack,” I think of someone feeling like they can’t breathe or like something terrible is happening right that second, and I haven’t really had that. There might be moments where I feel tense or overwhelmed, but it usually doesn’t come out of nowhere, and it doesn’t get to that level where I’d call it panic.\n\nSo… no, I wouldn’t say I’m frequently on the verge of that. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you've been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably where I’ve noticed it the most, honestly. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house just feels a lot more crowded and tense, or maybe I’m the one who’s more tense, I don’t know. I’ve been more on edge than usual, and I catch myself snapping over really small things—like noise, or someone leaving something out, or just being asked to do one more thing when I already feel like I’m trying to keep up.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve had some huge falling-out with my family or anything. I’m still there, I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do for the most part, but I can tell I’m shorter with people. Then I feel bad afterward, because it’s not really about them, or not completely. It’s just like I’m already drained before the day even starts sometimes.\n\nWith friends, I haven’t totally disappeared, but I’ve backed out of stuff more. Like I was supposed to help with the theater set, and I ended up not doing it because I couldn’t keep everything straight between home, class, and the shop. I’m still getting by, but I’ve missed a couple deadlines, and I think people can probably tell I’m not as reliable or easygoing as I usually am. So yeah, relationships haven’t fallen apart, but things feel strained, mostly because I’m more irritable and worn out.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I’ve lost interest, exactly. Like, I still like the same things when I actually get around to doing them, if that makes sense. It’s more that I sometimes don’t have the energy or the mental space to start, so from the outside it might look like I’m not interested.\n\nLike with stuff I normally enjoy, I don’t think I suddenly stopped caring about it or anything. If I’m there and I’m doing it, I can still enjoy it. It’s just been harder to make myself show up for things or commit to them, because everything else feels like it’s taking up room in my head.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think I’ve lost interest in things. Maybe I’ve been less active with them lately, but I wouldn’t say they stopped mattering to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I wouldn’t say there’s been some obvious, major sleep problem that I can point to, like “yes, every night I’m awake until 3 a.m.” or anything like that. I haven’t been tracking it or paying super close attention, honestly.\n\nSome nights I probably take longer to settle down, but I also feel like that happens to everyone sometimes, so I don’t know if I’d call it trouble sleeping. And then other nights I sleep fine, or at least I think I do. I wake up and I’m not always feeling amazing, but I don’t know if that’s sleep or just being tired in general.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed a clear sleep issue. If it’s there, it’s not something I’ve been aware of in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things that have happened or things you've done?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “guilty” is the main thing, no. I mean, I feel bad sometimes after I snap at somebody or if I don’t follow through on something, because I know that can affect other people. So in that sense, yeah, I’ll replay it a little and think, “Why did I act like that?” or “I should’ve handled that better.”\n\nBut I don’t walk around feeling guilty about big things from the past or like everything is my fault. It’s more like normal regret in the moment, I guess. I can usually tell myself, okay, I was stressed, I still need to apologize or do better, but I’m not sitting there thinking I’m a terrible person or anything like that. So… not often, not in a way that feels like it’s taking over.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble concentrating or making decisions in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. Not like I can’t function at all, but I’ve noticed I’m more scattered than I usually am. Since my sister and her baby moved back home, the house has just felt more hectic, and I think I’m carrying that with me. I’ll leave already kind of on edge or drained, and then by the time I’m in class or at the shop, I’m not as sharp.\n\nLike I can still get through the day, but I’m making more small mistakes or forgetting what I was supposed to do next. I’ve missed a couple deadlines, which isn’t really like me, at least not when I have things under control. It’s just taking more effort than it should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're often apprehensive or fearful about everyday situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I’d say I’m more apprehensive than I used to be, but not like I’m scared of normal things out of nowhere. Not bad exactly, but louder, more crowded, more unpredictable. I’ll be at home and something small happens, and I snap or get irritated way faster than I normally would. I’d wanted to help, and I normally like doing that kind of thing, but I just couldn’t keep up with everything I already had going on. It started feeling like one more thing I was going to mess up or disappoint people with, so I backed out.\n\nRelationship-wise, I wouldn’t say I’m losing people or anything, but I’m probably less available than usual. I’m mostly just trying to get through class and work and not fall further behind. So yeah, it’s affecting daily tasks more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're back to your normal self when you're away from home and not dealing with the stressors related to your sister and her baby?\n\nPatient: Not exactly, no.
